Then
M_{2}[y/x]=M_{2}
and
\Gamma_{2}[y/x]=\Gamma_{2}
.

So
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/x])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2})=(\Gamma_{2}[y/x],\tau_{2})
.

By induction,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/x])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/x],\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})
.

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm,

\mathsf{T}((M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x]))=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}[y/x])+(\Gamma_{2}[y/x])),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

And
M[y/x]=(M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x])
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/x])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}((M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x]))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}[y/x])+(\Gamma_{2}[y/x])),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})[y/x]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=((\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}))[y/x],\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma[y/x],\sigma).

iii.

x\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
and
x\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
:

Then
M_{1}[y/x]=M_{1}
and
\Gamma_{1}[y/x]=\Gamma_{1}
.

So
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/x])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})=(\Gamma_{1}[y/x],\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})
.

By induction,
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/x])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/x],\tau_{2})
.

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm,

\mathsf{T}((M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x]))=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}[y/x])+(\Gamma_{2}[y/x])),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

And
M[y/x]=(M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x])
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/x])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}((M_{1}[y/x])(M_{2}[y/x]))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}[y/x])+(\Gamma_{2}[y/x])),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\mathbb{S}((\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})[y/x]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=((\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}))[y/x],\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma[y/x],\sigma).

Any other possible case makes the left side of the implication (
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma,\sigma)
,
x\in\mathsf{FV}(M)
and
y
does not occur in
M
) false, which makes the statement true.
∎

Lemma 34
.

If
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma,\sigma)
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, and
y
does not occur in
M
, then
\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma)
, with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Proof.

By induction on the definition of
\mathsf{T}(M)
.

1.

If
M=\lambda x_{1}.M_{1}
and let
y
be a new variable not occurring in
M
, we have the following cases:

(a)

(\Gamma,\sigma)=({\Gamma_{1}}_{x_{1}},\Gamma_{1}(x_{1})\multimap\sigma_{1})
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, where
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
\Gamma_{1}(x_{1})=\tau\in\mathbb{T_{L}}_{0}
.

Since
{\Gamma_{1}}_{x_{1}}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, then
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\tau,y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

And since
y
does not occur in
M_{1}
(otherwise it would contradict the assumption that
y
does not occur in
M
),

by induction,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1})
,

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\tau,y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

So by rule 2.(b) of the inference algorithm,

\mathsf{T}(\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]))=({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})
.

And
{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}={\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}}
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

(b)

(\Gamma,\sigma)=({\Gamma_{1}}_{x_{1}},\Gamma_{1}(x_{1})\rightarrow\sigma_{1})
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, where
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\sigma_{1})
and
\Gamma_{1}(x_{1})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}
, with
n\geq 2
.

Since
{\Gamma_{1}}_{x_{1}}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, then
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

And since
y
does not occur in
M_{1}
(otherwise it would contradict the assumption that
y
does not occur in
M
),

by induction,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1})
,

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},x_{1}:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

So by rule 2.(c) of the inference algorithm,

\mathsf{T}(\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]))=({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}},\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1})
.

And
{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}={\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x_{1}}
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(\lambda x_{1}.(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1})

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

2.

If
M=M_{1}M_{2}
and let
y
be a new variable not occurring in
M
, we have the following cases:

(a)

(\Gamma,\sigma)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, where
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\alpha_{1})
,
\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2})
,
\mathbb{S}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{3},\tau_{2}=\alpha_{2}\})
and
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
do not occur in
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},\alpha_{1},\tau_{2}
.

Because
y
does not occur in
M
, then
y
does not occur in
M_{1}
nor in
M_{2}
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
M_{1}
is a term variable (otherwise its type given by the algorithm would not be a type variable), then there are five possible cases regarding the presence of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
in
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

i.

y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

So
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime},\tau\multimap\tau_{2}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

And since
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
,

so
M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\alpha_{1}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(a) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))
.

(Note that we can choose the same type variables
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
(and, consequently, the same
\mathbb{S}
) in
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])
because they are fresh in this inference and, since they do not occur in
\Gamma_{2}
, they also do not occur in
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}
(nor in
\Gamma_{1}
,
\alpha_{1},\tau_{2}
). For analogous reasons, the same can and will be done in the following cases.)

Since
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

ii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
:

So
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4},\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime},\tau_{2}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\alpha_{1})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
,

so
M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\alpha_{1}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(a) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})=(\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3})\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

iii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

iv.

y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\alpha_{1})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
,

so
M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\alpha_{1}).

(1)

Since
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{2})
.

And since
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}[y/y_{2}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{2}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(a) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}]),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}])\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}])
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

v.

y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

(b)

(\Gamma,\sigma)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, where
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime})
, with
n\geq 2
,
\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{i},\tau_{i})
for
1\leq i\leq n
, and
\mathbb{S}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(\{\tau_{i}=\tau_{i}^{\prime}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\})
.

Because
y
does not occur in
M
, then
y
does not occur in
M_{1}
nor in
M_{2}
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, then there are nine possible cases regarding the presence of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
in
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
(for all
1\leq i\leq n
):

i.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

And since
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
, by
28
,
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{i},\tau_{i}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(b) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

ii.

y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

iii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\Gamma_{i}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4_{i}})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4},\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}},\vec{\tau}_{4_{i}}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{n}})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
;

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime},\tau_{i}),

(2)

with
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{i}})
.

So by rule 3.(b) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{i}})
,
\Gamma_{i}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4_{i}})
,
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})\cap(\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{1}})\cap\cdots\cap(\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{n}})=(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}})\cap(\vec{\tau}_{4}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{4_{n}})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

iv.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\Gamma_{i}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4},\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{i})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{i}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(b) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}]\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}})
,
\Gamma_{i}\equiv(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3_{i}})
,
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}}=(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{1}}\cap\cdots\cap\vec{\tau}_{3_{n}})\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}])\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{1}])
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

v.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

vi.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

vii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

viii.

y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
,

so
M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime}).

(1)

Since
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{i})
.

And since
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}[y/y_{2}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{i}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(b) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{2}]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{2}])\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1}]+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}[y/y_{2}])
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

ix.

y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

(c)

(\Gamma,\sigma)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, where
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})
,
\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2})
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(\{\tau_{2}=\tau\})
.

Because
y
does not occur in
M
, then
y
does not occur in
M_{1}
nor in
M_{2}
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, then there are nine possible cases regarding the presence of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
in
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

i.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

And since
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

ii.

y_{1},y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

iii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4},\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime},\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
;

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime},\tau_{2}),

(2)

with
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})
.

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})\cap(\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})=(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})\cap(\vec{\tau}_{4}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}^{\prime})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime\prime})
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

iv.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

So
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
and
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
(for some
\vec{\tau}_{3},\vec{\tau}_{4},\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}(\vec{\tau}_{4})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
).

By induction,

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}),

(1)

with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
.

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{2})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}],\tau_{2}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

Since
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{1}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{4})
,
\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}]\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{2}\equiv(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
and
(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{4})\cap\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime}=(\vec{\tau}_{3}\cap\vec{\tau}_{3}^{\prime})\cap\vec{\tau}_{4}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}])\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{1}])
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

v.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

vi.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

vii.

y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

viii.

y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Since
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\tau\multimap\sigma_{1})
.

And since
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})
,

so
M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1}])=(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}],\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}).

(1)

Since
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
.

So by
33
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{2})
.

And since
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
, by
28
,
y_{1}\notin\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})
,

so
M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{2}[y/y_{2}]

and then

\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=\mathsf{T}(M_{2}[y/y_{2}])=(\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}],\tau_{2}).

(2)

So by rule 3.(c) of the inference algorithm (and (
1
), (
2
)),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}]),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))
.

Since
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
,

we have
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}])\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

Let
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}=\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}[y/y_{1}]+\Gamma_{2}[y/y_{2}])
.

Also,
M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, so

\displaystyle\mathsf{T}(M[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]M_{2}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}))

\displaystyle=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma),

with
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})
.

ix.

y_{1}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
y_{1}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
and
y_{2}\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
:

Analogous to the previous case.

Any other possible case makes the left side of the implication (
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma,\sigma)
, with
\Gamma\equiv(\Gamma^{\prime},y_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},y_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2})
, and
y
does not occur in
M
) false, which makes the statement true.
∎

Theorem 35

(Completeness)
.

If
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash_{2}M:\sigma
, then
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
(for some environment
\Gamma^{\prime}
and type
\sigma^{\prime}
) and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

Proof.

By induction on
|\Phi|
.

1.

(Axiom)
: Then
\Gamma=[x:\tau]
,
M=x
and
\sigma=\tau
.

\mathsf{T}(M)=([x:\alpha],\alpha)
and let
\mathbb{S}=[\tau/\alpha]
.

Then
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}(\alpha)=\alpha[\tau/\alpha]=\tau=\sigma

and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}([x:\alpha])=[x:\alpha[\tau/\alpha]]=[x:\tau]=\Gamma\equiv\Gamma
.

2.

(Exchange)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}
,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
holds.

By the induction hypothesis,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})

and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})
.

By definition,
(\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},\Gamma_{2})
.

So
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},y:\vec{\tau}_{2},x:\vec{\tau}_{1},\Gamma_{2})=\Gamma
.

And
\mathsf{T}(M)=\mathsf{T}(M_{1})
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}=\sigma
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
,

we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

3.

(Contraction)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},x_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
holds.

By the induction hypothesis,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})

and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},x_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})
.

Because
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},x_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})
, then
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})=\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},x_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})
.

So
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{3},x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1}^{\prime},x_{2}:\vec{\tau}_{2}^{\prime})
for some environment
\Gamma_{3}
and types
\vec{\tau}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\vec{\tau}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\vec{\tau}_{1}^{\prime})=\vec{\tau}_{1}
,
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\vec{\tau}_{2}^{\prime})=\vec{\tau}_{2}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{3})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
.

Then by
34
(
x
does not occur in
M_{1}
),

\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})
, with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{3},x:\vec{\tau}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2}^{\prime})
.

And
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{3},x:\vec{\tau}_{1}^{\prime}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2}^{\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2},x:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\Gamma_{2})=\Gamma
.

Also,
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
, so
\mathsf{T}(M)=\mathsf{T}(M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}])=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

4.

(
\rightarrow
Intro)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
\sigma=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
(with
n\geq 2
) holds.

By the induction hypothesis,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})

and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})
.

There is only one possible case for
\mathsf{T}(M)
:

•

(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime})\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
(with
m\geq 2
). Then
\mathsf{T}(M)=({\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime})
.

By
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})
and the assumption that
(x:\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime})\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}
.

Then by that and by
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
.

By
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})
and the definition of environment,
{\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x})\equiv\Gamma_{1}
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}={\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x}
,
\sigma^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

Note that there is not the case where
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
because by
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
(with
n\geq 2
) and
29
,
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.

There is also not the case where
(x:\tau)\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, as the substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
could not exist (because there is no substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau)=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}
).

5.

(
\rightarrow
Elim)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premises
\Gamma_{0}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{i}\vdash_{2}M_{2}:\tau_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
(with
n\geq 2
), hold.

By the induction hypothesis,

•

\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},\sigma_{0}^{\prime})
and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{0}
;

•

\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},\sigma_{i}^{\prime})
and there are substitutions
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\sigma_{i}^{\prime})=\tau_{i}
and
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{i}
, for
1\leq i\leq n
.

There is only one possible case for
\mathsf{T}(M)
:

•

\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3}
and, for each
1\leq i\leq n
,
\sigma_{i}^{\prime}=\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime}
:

Let
P=\{\tau_{i}^{\prime}=\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
.

Let us assume, without loss of generality, that
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},\sigma_{0}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
and all
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},\sigma_{i}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common (if they did, we could simply rename the type variables in each of the
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},\sigma_{i}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}
to fresh type variables and we would have the same result, as we consider types equal up to renaming of variables).

We have
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\sigma_{i}^{\prime})=\tau_{i}
and
\sigma_{i}^{\prime}=\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime}
, so
\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\tau_{i}
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3}
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
.

Equivalently,
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime})\cap\cdots\cap\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{n}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
.

So
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{i}^{\prime})=\tau_{i}
, for each
1\leq i\leq n
.

Then
\mathbb{S}_{3}=\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\cdots\cup\mathbb{S}_{n}^{\prime}
is a solution to
P
:

for all
1\leq i\leq n
,
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\tau_{i}^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{i}^{\prime})=\tau_{i}=\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{3}(\tau_{i}^{\prime\prime})
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(P)
.

Then we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3}))
, given by the algorithm.

By
17
of most general unifier, there exists an
\mathbb{S}
such that

(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})))=(\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})).

(1)

And
(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})))
is also a solution to
\mathsf{T}(M)
.

We have
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{0})\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})=\emptyset
, for all
1\leq i\leq n
(otherwise
\Gamma=\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
would be inconsistent),

so
\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\equiv\Gamma_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
.

Because of that and our initial assumption that
\Gamma_{0}^{\prime},\sigma_{0}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
and all
\Gamma_{i}^{\prime},\sigma_{i}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common, we have
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime})+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{S}_{i}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime}))\equiv\Gamma_{0},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
.

Finally, we have
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})=\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})
and
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3}))=\sigma_{1}
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{0}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}^{\prime})
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
and there is an
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

Note that there is not the case where
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3}
with
m\neq n
, as the substitution
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
could not exist (because there is no substitution
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
, for
m\neq n
).

There is also not the case where
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma_{3}
nor the case where
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}=\alpha
as the substitution
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}
(such that
\mathbb{S}_{0}^{\prime}(\sigma_{0}^{\prime})=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}
) could not exist.

6.

(
\multimap
Intro)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
\sigma=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
holds.

By the induction hypothesis,
\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\sigma^{\prime\prime})

and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau)
.

There is only one possible case for
\mathsf{T}(M)
:

•

(x:\tau^{\prime})\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
. Then
\mathsf{T}(M)=({\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x},\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma^{\prime\prime})
.

By
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau)
and the assumption that
(x:\tau^{\prime})\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})=\tau
.

Then by that and by
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\sigma_{1}
,

we have
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma^{\prime\prime})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
.

By
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})\equiv(\Gamma_{1},x:\tau)
and the definition of environment,
{\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x})\equiv\Gamma_{1}
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}={\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}_{x}
,
\sigma^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma^{\prime\prime}
and
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
,
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

Note that there is not the case where
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
because by
\Gamma_{1},x:\tau\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\sigma_{1}
and
29
,
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})
.

There is also not the case where
(x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})\in\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
, as the substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
could not exist (because there is no substitution
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})=\tau
).

7.

(
\multimap
Elim)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
\sigma=\sigma_{1}
, and assuming that the premises
\Gamma_{1}\vdash_{2}M_{1}:\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash_{2}M_{2}:\tau
hold.

By the induction hypothesis,

•

\mathsf{T}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime})
and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
and
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{1}
;

•

\mathsf{T}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime})
and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\sigma_{2}^{\prime})=\tau
and
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{2}
.

There are two possible cases for
\mathsf{T}(M)
:

•

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\alpha_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
:

Let
P=\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{3},\tau_{2}=\alpha_{2}\}
, where
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
are fresh variables.

Let us assume, without loss of generality, that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common (if they did, we could simply rename the type variables in
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
to fresh type variables and we would have the same result, as we consider types equal up to renaming of variables).

We have
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\sigma_{2}^{\prime})=\tau
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
, so
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\tau_{2})=\tau
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\alpha_{1}
, so
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\alpha_{1})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
.

Then
\mathbb{S}_{3}=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}\cup[\tau/\alpha_{2},\sigma_{1}/\alpha_{3}]
is a solution to
P
:

–

\mathbb{S}_{3}(\alpha_{1})=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\alpha_{1})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}=(\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{3})[\tau/\alpha_{2},\sigma_{1}/\alpha_{3}]=\mathbb{S}_{3}(\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{3})
;

–

\mathbb{S}_{3}(\tau_{2})=\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\tau_{2})=\tau=\alpha_{2}[\tau/\alpha_{2},\sigma_{1}/\alpha_{3}]=\mathbb{S}_{3}(\alpha_{2})
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(P)
.

Then we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\alpha_{3}))
, given by the algorithm.

By
17
of most general unifier, there exists an
\mathbb{S}
such that

(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\alpha_{3})))=(\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}_{3}(\alpha_{3})).

(1)

And
(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\alpha_{3})))
is also a solution to
\mathsf{T}(M)
.

We have
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})=\emptyset
(otherwise
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
would be inconsistent),

so
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\equiv\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.

Because of that and our initial assumption that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common, we have
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}))\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
.

Finally, we have
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\alpha_{3})=\sigma_{1}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\alpha_{3}))=\sigma_{1}
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\alpha_{3})
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
and there is an
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

•

\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma_{3}
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
:

Let
P=\{\tau_{2}=\tau^{\prime}\}
.

Let us assume, without loss of generality, that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common (if they did, we could simply rename the type variables in
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
to fresh type variables and we would have the same result, as we consider types equal up to renaming of variables).

We have
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\sigma_{2}^{\prime})=\tau
and
\sigma_{2}^{\prime}=\tau_{2}
, so
\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\tau_{2})=\tau
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma_{3}
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime}\multimap\sigma_{3})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
.

Equivalently,
(\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime}))\multimap(\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3}))=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
.

So
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})=\tau
and
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
.

Then
\mathbb{S}_{3}=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}\cup\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
is a solution to
P
:

\mathbb{S}_{3}(\tau_{2})=\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\tau_{2})=\tau=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\tau^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{3}(\tau^{\prime})
.

Let
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathsf{UNIFY}(P)
.

Then we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3}))
, given by the algorithm.

By
17
of most general unifier, there exists an
\mathbb{S}
such that

(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})))=(\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}),\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})).

(1)

And
(\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})),\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})))
is also a solution to
\mathsf{T}(M)
.

We have
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cap\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})=\emptyset
(otherwise
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
would be inconsistent),

so
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\equiv\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
.

Because of that and our initial assumption that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sigma_{1}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sigma_{2}^{\prime},\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}
do not have type variables in common, we have
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
.

And
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})+\mathbb{S}_{2}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}))\equiv\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
.

Finally, we have
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})=\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})
and
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
,

so
\mathbb{S}_{3}(\sigma_{3})=\sigma_{1}
,

which, by (
1
), is equivalent to
\mathbb{S}(\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3}))=\sigma_{1}
.

So for
\Gamma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
and
\sigma^{\prime}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}(\sigma_{3})
, we have
\mathsf{T}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime})
and there is an
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv\Gamma
.

Note that there is not the case where
\sigma_{1}^{\prime}=\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3}
, as the substitution
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
could not exist (because there is no substitution
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}
such that
\mathbb{S}_{1}^{\prime}(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{3})=\tau\multimap\sigma_{1}
).

∎

Hence, we end up with a sound and complete type inference algorithm for the Linear Rank 2 Intersection Type System.

3.4
Remarks

A
\lambda
-term
M
is called a
\lambda I
-term if and only if, for each subterm of the form
\lambda x.N
in
M
,
x
occurs free in
N
at least once. Note that our type system and type inference algorithm only type
\lambda I
-terms, but we could have extended them for the
affine terms
– a
\lambda
-term
M
is affine if and only if, for each subterm of the form
\lambda x.N
in
M
,
x
occurs free in
N
at most once, and if each free variable of
M
has just one occurrence free in
M
.

There is no unique and final way of typing affine terms. For instance, in the systems in
[
1
]
, arguments that do not occur in the body of the function get the empty type
[\;]
. Since we do not allow the empty sequence in our definition and adding it would make the system more complex, we decided to only work with
\lambda I
-terms.

Regarding our choice of defining environments as lists and having the rules (Exchange) and (Contraction) in the type system, instead of defining environments as sets and using the
(+)
operation for concatenation, that decision had to do with the fact that, this way, the system is closer to a linear type system. In the Linear Rank 2 Intersection Type System, a term is linear until we need to contract variables, so using these definitions makes us have more control over linearity and non-linearity. Also, it makes the system more easily extensible for other algebraic properties of intersection. We could also have rewritten the rule (
\rightarrow
Elim) in order not to use the
(+)
operation, which is something we might do in the future.

The downside of choosing these definitions is that it makes the proofs (in
section 3
and
section 4
) more complex, as they are not syntax directed because of the rules (Exchange) and (Contraction).

4
Resource Inference

Given the quantitative properties of the Linear Rank 2 Intersection Types, we now aim to redefine the type system and the type inference algorithm, in order to infer not only the type of a
\lambda
-term, but also parameters related to resource usage. In this case, we are interested in obtaining the number of evaluation steps of the
\lambda
-term to its normal form, for the leftmost-outermost strategy.

4.1
Type System

The new type system defined in this chapter results from an adaptation and merge between our Linear Rank 2 Intersection Type System (
11
) and the system for the leftmost-outermost evaluation strategy presented in
[
1
]
, as that system is able to derive a measure related to the number of evaluation steps for the leftmost-outermost strategy. We then begin by adapting some definitions from
[
1
]
and others that were already introduced in
section 3
.

The predicates
\mathsf{normal}
and
\mathsf{neutral}
defining, respectively, the leftmost-outermost normal terms and neutral terms, are in
36
. The predicate
\mathsf{abs}(M)
is true if and only if
M
is an abstraction;
\mathsf{normal(M)}
means that
M
is in normal form; and
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
means that
M
is in normal form and can never behave as an abstraction, i.e., it does not create a redex when applied to an argument.

Definition 36

(Leftmost-outermost normal forms)
.

\mathsf{neutral}(x)

\mathsf{neutral}(M)

\mathsf{normal}(N)

\mathsf{neutral}(MN)

\mathsf{neutral}(M)

\mathsf{normal}(M)

\mathsf{normal}(M)

\mathsf{normal}(\lambda x.M)

Definition 37

(Leftmost-outermost evaluation strategy)
.

(\lambda x.M)N\longrightarrow M[N/x]

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}

\lambda x.M\longrightarrow\lambda x.M^{\prime}

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}

\neg\mathsf{abs}(M)

MN\longrightarrow M^{\prime}N

\mathsf{neutral}(N)

M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}

NM\longrightarrow NM^{\prime}

Definition 38

(Finite rank multi-types)
.

We define the finite rank multi-types by the following grammar:

\displaystyle\mathsf{tight}

\displaystyle\Coloneqq\mathsf{Neutral}\mid\mathsf{Abs}

(Tight constants)

\displaystyle t

\displaystyle\Coloneqq\mathsf{tight}\mid\alpha\mid t\multimap t

(Rank 0 multi-types)

\displaystyle\vec{t}

\displaystyle\Coloneqq t\mid\vec{t}\cap\vec{t}

(Rank 1 multi-types)

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle\Coloneqq t\mid\vec{t}\rightarrow s

(Rank 2 multi-types)

Definition 39
.

•

Here, a
statement
is an expression of the form
M:(\vec{\tau},\vec{t})
, where the pair
(\vec{\tau},\vec{t})
is called the
predicate
, and the term
M
is called the
subject
of the statement.

•

A
declaration
is a statement where the subject is a term variable.

•

The comma operator (,) appends a declaration to the end of a list (of declarations). The list
(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
is the list that results from appending the list
\Gamma_{2}
to the end of the list
\Gamma_{1}
.

•

A finite list of declarations is
consistent
if and only if the term variables are all distinct.

•

An
environment
is a consistent finite list of declarations which predicates are pairs with a sequence from
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{1}
as the first element and a rank 1 multi-type as the second element of the pair (i.e., the declarations are of the form
x:(\vec{\tau},\vec{t})
), and we use
\Gamma
(possibly with single quotes and/or number subscripts) to range over environments.

•

An environment
\Gamma=[x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\dots,x_{n}:(\vec{\tau}_{n},\vec{t}_{n})]
induces a partial function
\Gamma
with domain
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)=\{x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\}
, and
\Gamma(x_{i})=(\vec{\tau}_{i},\vec{t}_{i})
.

•

We write
\Gamma_{x}
for the resulting environment of eliminating the declaration of
x
from
\Gamma
(if there is no declaration of
x
in
\Gamma
, then
\Gamma_{x}=\Gamma
).

•

We write
\Gamma_{1}\equiv\Gamma_{2}
if the environments
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
are equal up to the order of the declarations.

•

If
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
are environments, the environment
\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}
is defined as follows:

for each
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\cup\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})
,

(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2})(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Gamma_{1}(x)&\text{if }x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\\
\Gamma_{2}(x)&\text{if }x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})\\
(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2})&\text{if }\Gamma_{1}(x)=(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1})\text{ and }\Gamma_{2}(x)=(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2})\end{array}\right.

with the declarations of the variables in
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
in the beginning of the list, by the same order they appear in
\Gamma_{1}
, followed by the declarations of the variables in
\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2})\setminus\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
, by the order they appear in
\Gamma_{2}
.

•

We write
\mathsf{tight}(s)
if
s
is of the form
\mathsf{tight}
and
\mathsf{tight}(t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})
if
\mathsf{tight}(t_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
.

For
\Gamma=[x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\dots,x_{n}:(\vec{\tau}_{n},\vec{t}_{n})]
, we write
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
if
\mathsf{tight}(\vec{t}_{i})
for all
1\leq i\leq n
, in which case we also say that
\Gamma
is tight.

Definition 40

(Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System)
.

In the Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System, we say that
M
has type
\sigma
and multi-type
s
given the environment
\Gamma
, with index
b
, and write
\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
, if it can be obtained from the
derivation rules
in
Figure 3
.

[x:(\tau,t)]\vdash^{0}x:(\tau,t)

(Axiom)

\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)

\Gamma_{1},y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)

(Exchange)

\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),x_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)

\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]:(\sigma,s)

(Contraction)

\Gamma,x:(\tau,t)\vdash^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b}M:(\sigma,s)

\Gamma\vdash^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b+1}\lambda x.M:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

(
\multimap
Intro)

\Gamma,x:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,\mathsf{tight})

\Gamma\vdash^{b}\lambda x.M:(\tau\multimap\sigma,\mathsf{Abs})

(
\multimap
Intro
t
)

\Gamma,x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\vdash^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b}M:(\sigma,s)

n\geq 2

\Gamma\vdash^{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b+1}\lambda x.M:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}\rightarrow s)

(
\rightarrow
Intro)

\Gamma,x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},\vec{t})\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,\mathsf{tight})

\mathsf{tight}(\vec{t})

n\geq 2

\Gamma\vdash^{b}\lambda x.M:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,\mathsf{Abs})

(
\rightarrow
Intro
t
)

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}M_{2}:(\tau,t)

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}+b_{2}}M_{1}M_{2}:(\sigma,s)

(
\multimap
Elim)

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})

\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}M_{2}:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}+b_{2}}M_{1}M_{2}:(\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})

(
\multimap
Elim
t
)

\Gamma\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}\rightarrow s)

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{2}:(\tau_{1},t_{1})\text{ }\cdots\text{ }\Gamma_{n}\vdash^{b_{n}}M_{2}:(\tau_{n},t_{n})

n\geq 2

\Gamma,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}M_{2}:(\sigma,s)

(
\rightarrow
Elim)

\Gamma\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})

\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{2}:(\tau_{1},\mathsf{tight})\text{ }\cdots\text{ }\Gamma_{n}\vdash^{{b_{n}}}M_{2}:(\tau_{n},\mathsf{tight})

n\geq 2

\Gamma,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}M_{2}:(\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})

(
\rightarrow
Elim
t
)

Figure 3
:
Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System

The tight rules (the t-indexed ones) are used to introduce the tight constants
\mathsf{Neutral}
and
\mathsf{Abs}
, and they are related to minimal typings. Note that the index is only incremented in rules (
\multimap
Intro) and (
\rightarrow
Intro), as these are used to type abstractions that will be applied, contrary to the abstractions typed with the constant
\mathsf{Abs}
.

Notation
.

We write
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
if
\Phi
is a derivation tree ending with
\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
. In this case,
|\Phi|
is the depth of the derivation tree
\Phi
.

Definition 41

(Tight derivations)
.

A derivation
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
is tight if
\mathsf{tight}(s)
and
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
.

Similarly to what has been done in
[
1
]
, in this section we prove that, in the Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System, whenever a term is tightly typable with index
b
, then
b
is exactly the number of evaluations steps to leftmost-outermost normal form.

Example 42
.

Let
M=(\lambda x_{1}.(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1})I
, where
I
is the identity function
\lambda y.y
.

Let us first consider the leftmost-outermost evaluation of
M
to normal form:

(\lambda x_{1}.(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1})I\longrightarrow(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}I)I\longrightarrow II\longrightarrow I

So the evaluation sequence has length
3
.

Let us write
\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}
for the type
(\alpha\multimap\alpha)
and
\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}}
for the type
\mathsf{Abs}\multimap\mathsf{Abs}
.

To make the derivation tree easier to read, let us first get the following derivation
\Phi
for the term
\lambda x_{1}.(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1}
:

[x_{2}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})]\vdash^{0}x_{2}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})

[x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})]\vdash^{0}x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[x_{2}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}}),x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})]\vdash^{0}x_{2}x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})]\vdash^{1}\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{3}:((\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}})\multimap\;\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathsf{Abs})

[x_{4}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})]\vdash^{0}x_{4}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}}))

[x_{3}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs}),x_{4}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})]\vdash^{1}(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{3})x_{4}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[x_{1}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\cap\;(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}),\mathsf{Abs}\;\cap\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})]\vdash^{1}(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1}:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[\;]\vdash^{2}\lambda x_{1}.(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1}:((\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\cap\;(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}))\rightarrow\;\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},(\mathsf{Abs}\;\cap\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})\rightarrow\mathsf{Abs})

Then for the
\lambda
-term
M
, the following tight derivation is obtained:

\Phi

[y:(\alpha,\mathsf{Neutral})]\vdash^{0}y:(\alpha,\mathsf{Neutral})

[\;]\vdash^{0}I:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[y:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})]\vdash^{0}y:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

[\;]\vdash^{1}I:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}}\multimap\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathrel{\mathop{\mathsf{Abs}}\limits^{\vbox to1.80835pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\hbox{$-$}\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$-$}\kern-1.5pt\hbox{$\circ$}$}\vss}}})

[\;]\vdash^{3}(\lambda x_{1}.(\lambda x_{2}.x_{2}x_{1})x_{1})I:(\mathrel{\mathop{\alpha}\limits^{\vbox to0.18082pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\multimap$}\vss}}},\mathsf{Abs})

So indeed, the index
3
represents the number of evaluation steps to leftmost-outermost normal form.

We now show several properties of the type system, adapted from
[
1
]
, in order to prove the
tight correctness
(
Theorem 49
).

Lemma 43

(Tight spreading on neutral terms)
.

If
M
is a term such that
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
and
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
is a typing derivation such that
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, then
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

Proof.

By induction on
|\Phi|
.

Note that the last rule in
\Phi
cannot be any of the
\multimap
and
\rightarrow
intro ones, because
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
is not neutral.

1.

(Axiom)
: Then
\Gamma=[x:(\tau,t)]
,
M=x
and
s=t
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, then
t
is tight. So
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

2.

(Exchange)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
holds.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, and
(\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})\equiv\Gamma
, then
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})
. And since
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
and
M=M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
.

So by induction we get
\mathsf{tight}(s_{1})
. And because
s=s_{1}
, we have
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

3.

(Contraction)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming that the premise
\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),x_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
holds.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, and all types in
(\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),x_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})
appear in
\Gamma
, then
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),x_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})
. And
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
because
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}])
.

So by induction we get
\mathsf{tight}(s_{1})
. And because
s=s_{1}
, we have
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

4.

(
\multimap
Elim)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming that the premises
\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s_{1})
and
\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}M_{2}:(\tau,t)
hold.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}M_{2})
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
and
\mathsf{normal}(M_{2})

All types in
\Gamma_{1}
appear in
\Gamma
. Then since by hypothesis
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, we have
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma_{1})
.

Then we could apply the induction hypothesis to obtain
\mathsf{tight}(t\multimap s_{1})
, which is false.

So (
\multimap
Elim) cannot be the last rule in
\Phi
.

5.

(
\rightarrow
Elim)
: Similarly to (
\multimap
Elim), here we would obtain
\mathsf{tight}(t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}\rightarrow s_{1})
, so (
\rightarrow
Elim) also cannot be the last rule in
\Phi
.

6.

(
\multimap
Elim
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
and
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
.

Since
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, we already have
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

7.

(
\rightarrow
Elim
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma,\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
and
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
.

Since
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, we already have
\mathsf{tight}(s)
.

∎

Lemma 44

(Properties of tight typings for normal forms)
.

Let
M
be such that
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
be a typing derivation.

[(i)]

1.

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
b=0
.

2.

Neutrality
: if
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
then
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
.

Proof.

By induction on
|\Phi|
.

1.

(Axiom)
: Then
\Gamma=[x:(\tau,t)]
,
M=x
,
b=0
and
s=t
.

Clearly, both properties of the statement are verified in this case.

2.

(Exchange)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}
,
b=b_{1}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi_{1}
is tight and by induction,
b=b_{1}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
: if
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, since
s=s_{1}
,
s_{1}=\mathsf{Neutral}
and by induction,
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
. So since
M=M_{1}
,
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
.

3.

(Contraction)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
,
b=b_{1}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming
