\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),x_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi_{1}
is tight and by induction,
b=b_{1}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
: if
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, since
s=s_{1}
,
s_{1}=\mathsf{Neutral}
and by induction,
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
. So since
M=M_{1}[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
,
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
.

4.

(
\multimap
Intro)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
b=b_{1}+1
,
s=t\multimap s_{1}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
:
\Phi
is not tight, so the statement trivially holds.

(b)

Neutrality
:
s\neq\mathsf{Neutral}
, so the statement trivially holds.

5.

(
\rightarrow
Intro)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
b=b_{1}+1
,
s=t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}\rightarrow s_{1}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,s_{1})
, with
n\geq 2
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
:
\Phi
is not tight, so the statement trivially holds.

(b)

Neutrality
:
s\neq\mathsf{Neutral}
, so the statement trivially holds.

6.

(
\multimap
Intro
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
b=b_{1}
,
s=\mathsf{Abs}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,\mathsf{tight})
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi_{1}
is tight and by induction,
b=b_{1}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
:
s\neq\mathsf{Neutral}
, so the statement trivially holds.

7.

(
\rightarrow
Intro
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=\Gamma_{1}
,
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
,
b=b_{1}
,
s=\mathsf{Abs}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1},x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},\vec{t})\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\sigma,\mathsf{tight})
, with
\mathsf{tight}(\vec{t})
and
n\geq 2
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=\lambda x.M_{1}
, then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi_{1}
is tight and by induction,
b=b_{1}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
:
s\neq\mathsf{Neutral}
, so the statement trivially holds.

8.

(
\multimap
Elim)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
b=b_{1}+b_{2}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s_{1})
and
\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}M_{2}:(\tau,t)
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}M_{2}
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}M_{2})
. So
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
(and then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
) and
\mathsf{normal}(M_{2})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: this case is impossible. If
\Phi
is tight, then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
is tight, and so is
\Gamma_{1}
. And since
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
,
43
implies that the type of
M_{1}
in
\Phi_{1}
has to be tight, which is absurd.

(b)

Neutrality
:
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
holds by hypothesis.

9.

(
\rightarrow
Elim)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
,
s=s_{1}
, and assuming
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M_{1}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}\rightarrow s_{1})
and
\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, with
n\geq 2
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}M_{2}
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}M_{2})
. So
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
(and then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
) and
\mathsf{normal}(M_{2})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: this case is impossible. If
\Phi
is tight, then
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
is tight, and so is
\Gamma^{\prime}
. And since
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
,
43
implies that the type of
M_{1}
in
\Phi^{\prime}
has to be tight, which is absurd.

(b)

Neutrality
:
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
holds by hypothesis.

10.

(
\multimap
Elim
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
b=b_{1}+b_{2}
,
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, and assuming
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})
and
\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}M_{2}:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}M_{2}
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}M_{2})
. So
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
(and then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
) and
\mathsf{normal}(M_{2})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
are tight and by induction,
b_{1}=0
and
b_{2}=0
. So
b=b_{1}+b_{2}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
:
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
holds by hypothesis.

11.

(
\rightarrow
Elim
t
)
: Then
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
,
M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
,
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
, and assuming
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M_{1}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma,\mathsf{Neutral})
and
\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},\mathsf{tight})
, for
1\leq i\leq n
, with
n\geq 2
.

Since by hypothesis
\mathsf{normal}(M)
and
M=M_{1}M_{2}
, then
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1}M_{2})
. So
\mathsf{neutral}(M_{1})
(and then
\mathsf{normal}(M_{1})
) and
\mathsf{normal}(M_{2})
.

[(i)]

(a)

Tightness
: if
\Phi
is tight, then
\Phi^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
(for all
1\leq i\leq n
) are tight and by induction,
b^{\prime}=0
and
b_{i}=0
(for all
1\leq i\leq n
). So
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}=0
.

(b)

Neutrality
:
\mathsf{neutral}(M)
holds by hypothesis.

∎

Lemma 45

(Relevance)
.

If
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
, then
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)
if and only if
x\in\mathsf{FV}(M)
.

Proof.

Easy induction on
|\Phi|
.
∎

Lemma 46

(Substitution and typings)
.

Let
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s)
be a derivation with
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma(x)=(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})
, for
n\geq 1
. And, for each
1\leq i\leq n
, let
\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})
.

Then there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
. Moreover, if the derivations
\Phi,\Phi_{1},\dots,\Phi_{n}
are tight, then so is the derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The proof follows by induction on
|\Phi|
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
, so that
\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
is consistent. Otherwise, we could simply rename the free variables in
M_{1}
to get
M_{1}^{\prime}
(and the same derivation
\Phi
, with the variables renamed) such that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1}^{\prime})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
. Then, by a weaker form of the lemma (for
M_{1},M_{2}
such that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
), we would get the derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
(with the renamed variables) and finally we could apply the rule (Contraction) (and (Exchange), when necessary) to the variables that were renamed in
M_{1}
, in order to end up with the proper derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
.

1.

(Axiom)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd[x:(\tau_{1},t_{1})]\vdash^{0}x:(\tau_{1},t_{1}).

So
\Gamma=[x:(\tau_{1},t_{1})]
,
\Gamma_{x}=[\;]
,
M_{1}=x
,
b=0
,
\sigma=\tau_{1}
and
s=t_{1}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}M_{2}:(\tau_{1},t_{1})

Given that
(\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{1})=([\;],\Gamma_{1})=\Gamma_{1}
,
M_{1}[M_{2}/x]=x[M_{2}/x]=M_{2}
,
b+b_{1}=0+b_{1}=b_{1}
,
\sigma=\tau_{1}
and
s=t_{1}
, then we already have the derivation
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi_{1}
, as we wanted.

2.

(Exchange)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s),

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s)
.

And there are three different cases depending on
x
:

(a)

x\neq y_{1}
and
x\neq y_{2}
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{x}=({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})
.

Since
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma
, then either that declaration is in
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
or in
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
, and
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s).

By rule (Exchange), we get the final judgment we wanted:

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(b)

x=y_{1}
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
and
\Gamma_{x}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}:(\sigma,s)
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s),

which is the derivation we wanted.

(c)

x=y_{2}
:

Analogous to the case where
x=y_{1}
.

3.

(Contraction)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]:(\sigma,s),

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}^{\prime}:(\sigma,s)
.

And there are two different cases depending on
x
:

(a)

x\neq y
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})
,
M_{1}=M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
,
x\neq y_{1}
and
x\neq y_{2}
.

Since
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma
, then either that declaration is in
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
or in
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
, and
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s).

Note that this implies that
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
do not occur free in
M_{2}
, otherwise, by
45
,
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{i})
and so
{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}
would not be consistent.

By rule (Contraction), we get the final judgment we wanted:

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),y_{2}:(\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}(M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/x])[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]:(\sigma,s)

Since
x\neq y_{1}
,
x\neq y_{2}
,
x\neq y
and
y_{1},y_{2}
do not occur free in
M_{2}
,

then
(M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/x])[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=(M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])[M_{2}/x]
.

And as
M_{1}=M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]
, we have
(M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}])[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(b)

x=y
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
M_{1}=M_{1}^{\prime}[y/y_{1},y/y_{2}]=M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1},x/y_{2}]
and
x\neq y_{1}
and
x\neq y_{2}
(assuming that
y\neq y_{1}
and
y\neq y_{2}
, without loss of generality). Let us also assume, without loss of generality, that
y_{1},y_{2}
do not occur in
M_{2}
.

By hypothesis we have
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma
and
y:(\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2},\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2})\in\Gamma
.

So since
x=y
, we have
\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}=\vec{\tau}_{1}\cap\vec{\tau}_{2}
and
t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}=\vec{t}_{1}\cap\vec{t}_{2}
.

So for some
1\leq k<n
, we have
\vec{\tau}_{1}=\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k}
,
\vec{\tau}_{2}=\tau_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}
,
\vec{t}_{1}=t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k}
and
\vec{t}_{2}=t_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{1}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k}),y_{2}:(\tau_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b}M_{1}^{\prime}:(\sigma,s)
and
\Phi_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq k
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},y_{2}:(\tau_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{k+1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n}),\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Gamma_{j}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/y_{1}]:(\sigma,s).

Now given that derivation and
\Phi_{j}
for
k+1\leq j\leq n
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation

\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\Gamma_{j}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}+b_{k+1}+\dots+b_{n}}(M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/y_{1}])[M_{2}/y_{2}]:(\sigma,s),

which is the derivation we wanted.

Since
x\neq y_{1}
,
x\neq y_{2}
,
y_{1}\neq y_{2}
and
y_{1},y_{2},x
do not occur in
M_{2}
and
x
does not occur free in
M_{1}^{\prime}
, then:

\displaystyle(M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/y_{1}])[M_{2}/y_{2}]

\displaystyle=((M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1}])[M_{2}/x])[M_{2}/y_{2}]

\displaystyle=(((M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1}])[M_{2}/x])[x/y_{2}])[M_{2}/x]

\displaystyle=((M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1}])[x/y_{2}])[M_{2}/x]

\displaystyle=(M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1},x/y_{2}])[M_{2}/x]

And as
M_{1}=M_{1}^{\prime}[x/y_{1},x/y_{2}]
, we have
(M_{1}^{\prime}[M_{2}/y_{1}])[M_{2}/y_{2}]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})=\Gamma_{x}
and
b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}+b_{k+1}+\dots+b_{n}=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

4.

(
\multimap
Intro)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b^{\prime}+1}\lambda y.M:(\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime},t\multimap s^{\prime}),

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma,y:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M:(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime})
.

So
M_{1}=\lambda y.M
,
b=b^{\prime}+1
,
\sigma=\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime}
,
s=t\multimap s^{\prime}
and
x\neq y
.

Since
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma
, then
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in(\Gamma,y:(\tau,t))
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

\Gamma_{x},y:(\tau,t),\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}).

We can now perform consecutive applications of (Exchange) in order to get

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},y:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime}).

Finally, by rule (
\multimap
Intro), we get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},y:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},s^{\prime})

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+1}\lambda y.(M[M_{2}/x]):(\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime},t\multimap s^{\prime})

Since
M_{1}=\lambda y.M
and
x\neq y
, then
\lambda y.(M[M_{2}/x])=(\lambda y.M)[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
b=b^{\prime}+1
, so
b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+1=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

5.

(
\rightarrow
Intro)
:

Similar to the previous case.

6.

(
\multimap
Intro
t
)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b^{\prime}}\lambda y.M:(\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{Abs}),

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma,y:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M:(\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{tight})
.

So
M_{1}=\lambda y.M
,
b=b^{\prime}
,
\sigma=\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime}
,
s=\mathsf{Abs}
and
x\neq y
.

Since
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma
, then
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in(\Gamma,y:(\tau,\mathsf{tight}))
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

\Gamma_{x},y:(\tau,\mathsf{tight}),\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{tight}).

We can now perform consecutive applications of (Exchange) in order to get

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},y:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{tight}).

Finally, by rule (
\multimap
Intro
t
), we get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},y:(\tau,\mathsf{tight})\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{tight})

\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}\lambda y.(M[M_{2}/x]):(\tau\multimap\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{Abs})

Since
M_{1}=\lambda y.M
and
x\neq y
, then
\lambda y.(M[M_{2}/x])=(\lambda y.M)[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
b=b^{\prime}
, so
b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

7.

(
\rightarrow
Intro
t
)
:

Similar to the previous case.

8.

(
\multimap
Elim)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}}N_{1}N_{2}:(\sigma,s),

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)
and
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{2}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau,t)
.

Since
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
, either
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
(and
x\in\mathsf{FV}(N_{1})
, by
45
) or
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
(and
x\in\mathsf{FV}(N_{2})
, by
45
). Note that there is not the case where
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
simultaneously, otherwise
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
would be inconsistent.

So there are two different cases depending on
x
:

(a)

x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
and
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}
.

And
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s).

By rule (
\multimap
Elim), with
\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{2}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau,t)
from
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
, we get:

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{2}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau,t)

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{2}^{\prime}}(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}:(\sigma,s)

Note that
({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
is consistent because of our initial assumption that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
.

We can now perform consecutive applications of (Exchange) in order to get the final judgment we wanted:

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{2}^{\prime}}(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}:(\sigma,s)

Since
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
x\notin\mathsf{FV}(N_{2})
(by
45
, since
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
), then
(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}=(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}N_{2})[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}
, so
b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{2}^{\prime}=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(b)

x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
and
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x})
,
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}
.

And
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{2}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{2}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{2}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau,t).

By rule (
\multimap
Elim), with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)
from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
, we get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{2}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{2}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau,t)

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{1}(N_{2}[M_{2}/x]):(\sigma,s)

Note that
(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})
is consistent because of our initial assumption that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
.

Since
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
x\notin\mathsf{FV}(N_{1})
(by
45
, since
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime})
), then
N_{1}(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}N_{2})[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}
, so
b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

9.

(
\multimap
Elim
t
)
:

Similar to the previous case.

10.

(
\rightarrow
Elim)
:

Then we have

\Phi\rhd\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}}N_{1}N_{2}:(\sigma,s)

which follows from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rhd\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap t_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow s)
,

\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})
, …,
\Phi_{m}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{m}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{m}^{\prime},t_{m}^{\prime})
and
m\geq 2
.

Since
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma)
and
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
, either
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
(and
x\in\mathsf{FV}(N_{1})
, by
45
) or
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
, for
1\leq j\leq m
(and
x\in\mathsf{FV}(N_{2})
, by
45
). Note that there is not the case where
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
(for
1\leq j\leq m
) simultaneously, otherwise
(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
would be inconsistent.

So there are two different cases depending on
x
:

(a)

x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
and
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
, for
1\leq j\leq m
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=({\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
,
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}
.

And
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\Gamma^{\prime}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

Given
\Phi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
and
\Phi_{i}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap t_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow s).

By rule (
\rightarrow
Elim), with
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})
, …,
\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{m}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{m}^{\prime},t_{m}^{\prime})
from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
, …,
\Phi_{m}^{\prime}
, respectively, we get:

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap t_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow s)

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})\text{ }\cdots\text{ }\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{m}^{\prime}}N_{2}:(\tau_{m}^{\prime},t_{m}^{\prime})

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}}(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}:(\sigma,s)

Note that
({\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
is consistent because of our initial assumption that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
.

We can now perform consecutive applications of (Exchange) in order to get the final judgment we wanted:

{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}}(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}:(\sigma,s)

Since
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
x\notin\mathsf{FV}(N_{2})
(by
45
, since
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
for
1\leq j\leq m
), then
(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])N_{2}=(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}N_{2})[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}
, so
b^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}
.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(b)

x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
, for
1\leq j\leq m
, and
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
:

So
\Gamma=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
,
\Gamma_{x}=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}}_{x})
,
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}
.

And
x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})\in\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}
, which means that the sequences
(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{n})
can be split between the environments
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}
. Also, note that this implies
n\geq m
(because by
45
,
x\in\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{j}^{\prime})
for all
1\leq j\leq m
).

Then for
1=k_{1}<\dots<k_{m}<k_{m+1}=n+1
and
1\leq j\leq m
,

let
x:(\tau_{k_{j}}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k_{(j+1)}-1},t_{k_{j}}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k_{(j+1)}-1})\in\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}
.

By hypothesis we also have:

\Phi_{i}\rhd\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{i}}M_{2}:(\tau_{i},t_{i})

for
1\leq i\leq n
.

So for each
1\leq j\leq m
, given
\Phi_{j}^{\prime}
and
\Phi_{k_{j}},\dots,\Phi_{k_{(j+1)}-1}
, by the induction hypothesis, there is a derivation ending with

{\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i={k_{j}}}^{k_{(j+1)}-1}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{j}^{\prime}+b_{k_{j}}+\dots+b_{k_{(j+1)}-1}}N_{2}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau_{j}^{\prime},t_{j}^{\prime}).

By rule (
\rightarrow
Elim), with
\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap t_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow s)
from
\Phi_{1}^{\prime\prime}
, we get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b^{\prime}}N_{1}:(\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap t_{m}^{\prime}\rightarrow s)

{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k_{2}-1}}N_{2}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau_{1}^{\prime},t_{1}^{\prime})\text{ }\cdots\text{ }{\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i={k_{m}}}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{m}^{\prime}+b_{k_{m}}+\dots+b_{n}}N_{2}[M_{2}/x]:(\tau_{m}^{\prime},t_{m}^{\prime})

\Gamma^{\prime},(({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Gamma_{i})+\dots+({\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i={k_{m}}}^{n}\Gamma_{i}))\vdash^{b^{\prime\prime}}N_{1}(N_{2}[M_{2}/x]):(\sigma,s)

where
b^{\prime\prime}=b^{\prime}+(b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k_{2}-1})+\dots+(b_{m}^{\prime}+b_{k_{m}}+\dots+b_{n})
.

By our initial assumption that
\mathsf{FV}(M_{1})\cap\mathsf{FV}(M_{2})=\emptyset
, by
45
, and by looking at the definition of (+), we have:

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},(({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Gamma_{i})+\dots+({\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i={k_{m}}}^{n}\Gamma_{i}))

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},(({\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}_{x}+\dots+{\Gamma_{m}^{\prime}}_{x}),(\sum_{i=1}^{k_{2}-1}\Gamma_{i}+\dots+\sum_{i={k_{m}}}^{n}\Gamma_{i}))

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\prime},(\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}}_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\Gamma^{\prime},\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\Gamma_{j}^{\prime}}_{x}),\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}.

Since
M_{1}=N_{1}N_{2}
and
x\notin\mathsf{FV}(N_{1})
(by
45
, since
x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma^{\prime})
), then
N_{1}(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}[M_{2}/x])(N_{2}[M_{2}/x])=(N_{1}N_{2})[M_{2}/x]=M_{1}[M_{2}/x]
.

Also since
b=b^{\prime}+b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime}
, we have:

\displaystyle b^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=b^{\prime}+(b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k_{2}-1})+\dots+(b_{m}^{\prime}+b_{k_{m}}+\dots+b_{n})

\displaystyle=b^{\prime}+(b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime})+(b_{1}+\dots+b_{k_{2}-1}+\dots+b_{k_{m}}+\dots+b_{n})

\displaystyle=b^{\prime}+(b_{1}^{\prime}+\dots+b_{m}^{\prime})+(b_{1}+\dots+b_{n})

\displaystyle=b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}.

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{x},\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b+b_{1}+\dots+b_{n}}M_{1}[M_{2}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(
\rightarrow

t
)
:

Similar to the previous case.

∎

We now show an important property that relates contracted terms with their linear counterpart. Basically, it says that the following diagram commutes (under the described conditions):

M
N
M^{\prime}
N^{\prime}
\beta
\mathcal{S}(M^{\prime})
\beta
\mathcal{S}(N^{\prime})

Lemma 47
.

Let
M\longrightarrow N
and
M=\mathcal{S}(M^{\prime})
for some substitution
\mathcal{S}=[x/x_{1},x/x_{2}]
where
x_{1},x_{2}
occur free in
M^{\prime}
and
x
does not occur in
M^{\prime}
. Then there exists a term
N^{\prime}
such that
N=\mathcal{S}(N^{\prime})
and
M^{\prime}\longrightarrow N^{\prime}
.

Proof.

This trivially holds because
\mathcal{S}
simply renames free variables – suppose that in
M
we annotate each occurrence of
x
with the variable it substituted (so that each occurrence is either
x^{x_{1}}
or
x^{x_{2}}
). Then
M
is equal to
M^{\prime}
, up to renaming of variables.
∎

Convention 4.1
.

Without loss of generality, we assume that, in a derivation tree, all contracted variables (i.e., variables that, at some point in the derivation tree, disappear from the term and environment by an application of the (Contraction) rule) are different from any other variable in the derivation tree.

We also assume that when applying (Contraction), the new variables that substitute the contracted ones are also different from any other variable in the derivation tree.

Lemma 48

(Quantitative subject reduction)
.

If
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
is tight and
M\longrightarrow N
, then
b\geq 1
and there exists a tight derivation
\Phi^{\prime}
such that
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b-1}N:(\sigma,s)
.

Proof.

We prove the following stronger statement:

Assume
M\longrightarrow N
,
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
,
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
, and either
\mathsf{tight}(s)
or
\neg\mathsf{abs}(M)
.

Then there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b-1}N:(\sigma,s)
.

We prove this statement by induction on
M\longrightarrow N
.

1.

Rule

(\lambda x.M_{1})N_{1}\longrightarrow M_{1}[N_{1}/x]

:

Assume
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}(\lambda x.M_{1})N_{1}:(\sigma,s)
and
\mathsf{tight}(\Gamma)
.

So at some point in the derivation
\Phi
, either the rule (
\multimap
Elim) or (
\rightarrow
Elim) is applied (not (
\multimap
Elim
t
) nor (
\rightarrow
Elim
t
) because it is not possible to derive the type
\mathsf{Neutral}
for an abstraction) and is then followed by zero or more applications of the rules (Exchange) and/or (Contraction).

Case where the last rule different from (Exchange) and (Contraction) applied in
\Phi
is:

(a)

(
\multimap
Elim):

Then at some point in
\Phi
we have:

\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime}):(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau,t)

\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}+b_{2}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime})N_{1}^{\prime}:(\sigma,s)

Assume that after the application of this rule, the rule (Contraction) was applied
n
times (with
n\geq 0
) and (Exchange) zero or more times, and let
\mathcal{S}=[y_{n}/x_{n},y_{n}/x_{n}^{\prime}]\circ[y_{n-1}/x_{n-1},y_{n-1}/x_{n-1}^{\prime}]\circ\cdots\circ[y_{1}/x_{1},y_{1}/x_{1}^{\prime}]
be the substitution that reflects the
n
applications of the rule (Contraction). Then we have:

•

M_{1}=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime})
;

•

N_{1}=\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})
;

•

b=b_{1}+b_{2}
.

Since
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime}):(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)
, at some point in the derivation
\Phi_{1}
, the rule (
\multimap
Intro) is applied, followed by zero or more applications of the rules (Exchange) and/or (Contraction). So at some point in
\Phi_{1}
we have:

\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\sigma,s)

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+1}\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\tau\multimap\sigma,t\multimap s)

Assume that after the application of this rule, the rule (Contraction) was applied
m
times (with
m\geq 0
) and (Exchange) zero or more times, and let
\mathcal{S}_{1}=[y_{n}^{\prime}/z_{m},y_{n}^{\prime}/z_{m}^{\prime}]\circ[y_{m-1}^{\prime}/z_{m-1},y_{m-1}^{\prime}/z_{m-1}^{\prime}]\circ\cdots\circ[y_{1}^{\prime}/z_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}/z_{1}^{\prime}]
be the substitution that reflects the
m
applications of the rule (Contraction). Then we have:

•

M_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})
;

•

b_{1}=b_{1}^{\prime}+1
.

We can then apply
46
for the derivations
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},x:(\tau,t)\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\sigma,s)
and
\Phi_{2}\rhd\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{2}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau,t)
to obtain

\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]:(\sigma,s).

Note that we can assume
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\Gamma_{2}
to be consistent by
4.1
and the fact that
\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2}
is consistent.

If we perform the
m
applications of (Contraction) (and the necessary applications of (Exchange)) over the same variables over which they were performed in
\Phi_{1}
after the application of (
\multimap
Intro), i.e., over the variables
z_{1},z_{1}^{\prime},z_{2},z_{2}^{\prime},\dots,z_{m},z_{m}^{\prime}
, we can obtain:

\Gamma_{3}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}}\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]):(\sigma,s)

where
\Gamma_{3}^{\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1},\Gamma_{2})
. (By
4.1
, the variables in
\Gamma_{2}
are not substituted.)

If we now perform the
n
applications of (Contraction) (and the necessary applications of (Exchange)) over the same variables over which they were performed in
\Phi
after the application of (
\multimap
Elim), i.e., over the variables
x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2},x_{2}^{\prime},\dots,x_{n},x_{n}^{\prime}
, we can obtain:

\Gamma_{3}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])):(\sigma,s)

where
\Gamma_{3}\equiv\Gamma
.

Finally, since
\Gamma_{3}\equiv\Gamma
, we can apply (Exchange) as many times as needed to get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])):(\sigma,s)

Since
b=b_{1}+b_{2}
and
b_{1}=b_{1}^{\prime}+1
, then

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{2}

\displaystyle=b_{1}-1+b_{2}

\displaystyle=b-1.

By
4.1
, we have
\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])=(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}))[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]
.

Also,
M_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})
, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}))[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]

\displaystyle=M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x].

(1)

Because
x
cannot be in
\mathcal{S}
, then
\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])=(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]
.

And since
M_{1}=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime})
and
N_{1}=\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})
, we have
(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]=M_{1}[N_{1}/x]
, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]

\displaystyle=M_{1}[N_{1}/x].

(2)

Then, by (
1a
) and (
1a
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=M_{1}[N_{1}/x].

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b-1}M_{1}[N_{1}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

(b)

(
\rightarrow
Elim):

Then at some point in
\Phi
we have:

\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime}):(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k}\rightarrow s)

\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau_{1},t_{1})\text{ }\cdots\text{ }\Phi_{k}\rhd\Gamma_{k}\vdash^{b_{k}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau_{k},t_{k})

k\geq 2

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime})N_{1}^{\prime}:(\sigma,s)

Assume that after the application of this rule, the rule (Contraction) was applied
n
times (with
n\geq 0
) and (Exchange) zero or more times, and let
\mathcal{S}=[y_{n}/x_{n},y_{n}/x_{n}^{\prime}]\circ[y_{n-1}/x_{n-1},y_{n-1}/x_{n-1}^{\prime}]\circ\cdots\circ[y_{1}/x_{1},y_{1}/x_{1}^{\prime}]
be the substitution that reflects the
n
applications of the rule (Contraction). Then we have:

•

M_{1}=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime})
;

•

N_{1}=\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})
;

•

b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}
.

Since
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime}}(\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime}):(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k}\rightarrow s)
, at some point in the derivation
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
, the rule (
\rightarrow
Intro) is applied, followed by zero or more applications of the rules (Exchange) and/or (Contraction). So at some point in
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
we have:

\Phi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k})\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\sigma,s)

k\geq 2

\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+1}\lambda x.M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k}\rightarrow\sigma,t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k}\rightarrow s)

Assume that after the application of this rule, the rule (Contraction) was applied
m
times (with
m\geq 0
) and (Exchange) zero or more times, and let
\mathcal{S}_{1}=[y_{n}^{\prime}/z_{m},y_{n}^{\prime}/z_{m}^{\prime}]\circ[y_{m-1}^{\prime}/z_{m-1},y_{m-1}^{\prime}/z_{m-1}^{\prime}]\circ\cdots\circ[y_{1}^{\prime}/z_{1},y_{1}^{\prime}/z_{1}^{\prime}]
be the substitution that reflects the
m
applications of the rule (Contraction). Then we have:

•

M_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})
;

•

b_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+1
.

We can then apply
46
for the derivations
\Phi_{1}^{\prime\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},x:(\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{k},t_{1}\cap\cdots\cap t_{k})\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}:(\sigma,s)
and
\Phi_{1}\rhd\Gamma_{1}\vdash^{b_{1}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau_{1},t_{1}),\dots,\Phi_{k}\rhd\Gamma_{k}\vdash^{b_{k}}N_{1}^{\prime}:(\tau_{k},t_{k})
to obtain

\Phi^{\prime\prime}\rhd\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]:(\sigma,s).

Note that we can assume
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i}
to be consistent by
4.1
and the fact that
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i}
is consistent.

If we perform the
m
applications of (Contraction) (and the necessary applications of (Exchange)) over the same variables over which they were performed in
\Phi_{1}^{\prime}
after the application of (
\rightarrow
Intro), i.e., over the variables
z_{1},z_{1}^{\prime},z_{2},z_{2}^{\prime},\dots,z_{m},z_{m}^{\prime}
, we can obtain:

\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]):(\sigma,s)

where
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\equiv(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i})
. (By
4.1
, the variables in
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\Gamma_{i}
are not substituted.)

If we now perform the
n
applications of (Contraction) (and the necessary applications of (Exchange)) over the same variables over which they were performed in
\Phi
after the application of (
\rightarrow
Elim), i.e., over the variables
x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime},x_{2},x_{2}^{\prime},\dots,x_{n},x_{n}^{\prime}
, we can obtain:

\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])):(\sigma,s)

where
\Gamma^{\prime}\equiv\Gamma
.

Finally, since
\Gamma^{\prime}\equiv\Gamma
, we can apply (Exchange) as many times as needed to get the final judgment we wanted:

\Gamma\vdash^{b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}}\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])):(\sigma,s)

Since
b=b_{1}^{\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}
and
b_{1}^{\prime}=b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+1
, then

\displaystyle b_{1}^{\prime\prime}+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}

\displaystyle=b_{1}^{\prime}-1+b_{1}+\dots+b_{k}

\displaystyle=b-1.

By
4.1
, we have
\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])=(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}))[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]
.

Also,
M_{1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime})
, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}))[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]

\displaystyle=M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x].

(1)

Because
x
cannot be in
\mathcal{S}
, then
\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])=(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]
.

And since
M_{1}=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime})
and
N_{1}=\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})
, we have
(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]=M_{1}[N_{1}/x]
, so

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}))[\mathcal{S}(N_{1}^{\prime})/x]

\displaystyle=M_{1}[N_{1}/x].

(2)

Then, by (
1b
) and (
1b
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{S}_{1}(M_{1}^{\prime\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x]))

\displaystyle=\mathcal{S}(M_{1}^{\prime}[N_{1}^{\prime}/x])

\displaystyle=M_{1}[N_{1}/x].

So there is indeed
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b-1}M_{1}[N_{1}/x]:(\sigma,s)
.

∎

Theorem 49

(Tight correctness)
.

If
\Phi\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
is a tight derivation, then there exists
N
such that
M\longrightarrow^{b}N
and
\mathsf{normal}(N)
. Moreover, if
s=\mathsf{Neutral}
then
\mathsf{neutral}(N)
.

Proof.

By induction on the evaluation length of
M\longrightarrow^{k}N
.

If
M
is a (leftmost-outermost) normal form, then by taking
N=M
and
k=0
, the statement follows from the tightness property of tight typings of normal forms (
44
(i)). The
moreover
part follows from the neutrality property (
44
(ii)).

Otherwise,
M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}
and by quantitative subject reduction (
48
) there exists a derivation
\Phi^{\prime}\rhd\Gamma\vdash^{b-1}M^{\prime}:(\sigma,s)
. By induction, there exists
N
such that
\mathsf{normal}(N)
and
M^{\prime}\longrightarrow^{b-1}N
. Note that
M\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\longrightarrow^{b-1}N
, that is,
M\longrightarrow^{b}N
.
∎

4.2
Type Inference Algorithm

We now extend the type inference algorithm defined in
section 3
(
23
) to also infer the number of reduction steps of the typed term to its normal form, when using the leftmost-outermost evaluation strategy.

This is done by slightly modifying the unification algorithm in
21
and the algorithm in
23
, which will now carry and update a measure
b
that relates to the number of reduction steps. First, recall
20
, presented in
section 3
.

Definition 50

(Quantitative Unification Algorithm)
.

Let
P
be a unification problem (with types in
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{0}
). The new unification function
\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(P)
, which decides whether
P
has a solution and, if so, returns the MGU of
P
and an integer
b
used for counting purposes in the inference algorithm, is defined as:

function

\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}
(
P
)

b\coloneqq 0
;

while

P\Rightarrow P^{\prime}

do

if

P=\{\tau_{1}\multimap\tau_{2}=\tau_{3}\multimap\tau_{4}\}\cup P_{1}

and

P^{\prime}=\{\tau_{1}=\tau_{3},\tau_{2}=\tau_{4}\}\cup P_{1}

then

b\coloneqq b+1
;

P\coloneqq P^{\prime}
;

if

P
is in solved form
then

return

(\mathbb{S}_{P},b)
;

else

\mathsf{FAIL}
;

Let
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{1}
-environment be an environment as defined in
section 3
, i.e., just like the definition we use in the current chapter, but the predicates are only the first element of the pair (i.e., a sequence from
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{1}
).

Definition 51

(Quantitative Type Inference Algorithm)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{1}
-environment,
M
a
\lambda
-term,
\sigma
a linear rank 2 intersection type,
b
a quantitative measure and
\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}
the function in
50
. The function
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=(\Gamma,\sigma,b)
defines a new type inference algorithm that gives a quantitative measure for the
\lambda
-calculus in the Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System, in the following way:

1.

If

M=x
,
then

\Gamma=[x:\alpha]
,
\sigma=\alpha
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b=0}
, where
\alpha
is a new variable;

2.

If

M=\lambda x.M_{1}
and
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\sigma_{1},b_{1})

then
:

(a)

if

x\notin\mathsf{dom}(\Gamma_{1})
,
then

\mathsf{FAIL}
;

(b)

if

(x:\tau)\in\Gamma_{1}
,
then

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=({\Gamma_{1}}_{x},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b_{1}})
;

(c)

if

(x:\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n})\in\Gamma_{1}
(with
n\geq 2
),
then

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=({\Gamma_{1}}_{x},\tau_{1}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}\rightarrow\sigma_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b_{1}})
.

3.

If

M=M_{1}M_{2}
,
then
:

(a)

if

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\alpha_{1},b_{1})
and
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2},b_{2})
,

then

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{3}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b_{1}+b_{2}})
,

where
(\mathbb{S},\_)=\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{3},\tau_{2}=\alpha_{2}\})
and
\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}
are new variables;

(b)

if

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime},\tau_{1}^{\prime}\cap\cdots\cap\tau_{n}^{\prime}\rightarrow\sigma_{1}^{\prime},b_{1})
(with
n\geq 2
) and, for each
1\leq i\leq n
,
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{i},\tau_{i},b_{i})
,

then

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Gamma_{i}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}^{\prime}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b_{1}+\sum_{i=1}^{n}b_{i}+b_{3}+1})
,

where
(\mathbb{S},b_{3})=\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(\{\tau_{i}=\tau_{i}^{\prime}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\})
;

(c)

if

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{1})=(\Gamma_{1},\tau\multimap\sigma_{1},b_{1})
and
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M_{2})=(\Gamma_{2},\tau_{2},b_{2})
,

then

\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=(\mathbb{S}(\Gamma_{1}+\Gamma_{2}),\mathbb{S}(\sigma_{1}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}b_{1}+b_{2}+b_{3}+1})
,

where
(\mathbb{S},b_{3})=\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(\{\tau_{2}=\tau\})
;

(d)

otherwise

\mathsf{FAIL}
.

Note that
b
is only increased by
1
and added the quantity given by
\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}
in rules 3.(b) and 3.(c), since these are the only cases in which the term
M
is a redex.

Example 52
.

Let us show the type inference process for the
\lambda
-term
\lambda x.xx
.

•

By rule 1.,
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(x)=([x:\alpha_{1}],\alpha_{1},0)
.

•

By rule 1., again,
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(x)=([x:\alpha_{2}],\alpha_{2},0)
.

•

Then by rule 3.(a),
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(xx)=(\mathbb{S}([x:\alpha_{1}]+[x:\alpha_{2}]),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{4}),0+0)=(\mathbb{S}([x:\alpha_{1}\cap\alpha_{2}]),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{4}),0)
,

where
(\mathbb{S},\_)=\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}\})=([\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4}/\alpha_{1},\alpha_{3}/\alpha_{2}],0)
.

So
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(xx)=([x:(\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4})\cap\alpha_{3}],\alpha_{4},0)
.

•

Finally, by rule 2.(c),
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(\lambda x.xx)=([\;],(\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4})\cap\alpha_{3}\rightarrow\alpha_{4},0)
.

Example 53
.

Let us now show the type inference process for the
\lambda
-term
(\lambda x.xx)(\lambda y.y)
.

•

From the previous example, we have
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(\lambda x.xx)=([\;],(\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4})\cap\alpha_{3}\rightarrow\alpha_{4},0)
.

•

By rules 1. and 2.(b), for the identity, the algorithm gives
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(\lambda y.y)=([\;],\alpha_{1}\multimap\alpha_{1},0)
.

•

By rules 1. and 2.(b), again, for the identity,
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(\lambda y.y)=([\;],\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2},0)
.

•

Then by rule 3.(b),
\mathsf{T_{Q}}((\lambda x.xx)(\lambda y.y))=(\mathbb{S}([\;]+[\;]+[\;]),\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{4}),0+0+0+b_{3}+1)=([\;],\mathbb{S}(\alpha_{4}),b_{3}+1)
, where
(\mathbb{S},b_{3})=\mathsf{UNIFY_{Q}}(\{\alpha_{1}\multimap\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}\})
, calculated by performing the following transformations:

\displaystyle\{\alpha_{1}\multimap\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3}\multimap\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3},\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{3},\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{3}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}=\alpha_{4}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{4},\alpha_{4}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\{\alpha_{1}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2},\alpha_{3}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2},\alpha_{4}=\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2}\}

So
\mathbb{S}=[(\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2})/\alpha_{1},(\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2})/\alpha_{3},(\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2})/\alpha_{4}]

and
b_{3}=1
because there was performed one transformation (the first) of the form
\{\tau_{1}\multimap\tau_{2}=\tau_{3}\multimap\tau_{4}\}\cup P\Rightarrow\{\tau_{1}=\tau_{3},\tau_{2}=\tau_{4}\}\cup P
.

And then,
\mathsf{T_{Q}}((\lambda x.xx)(\lambda y.y))=([\;],\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2},1+1)=([\;],\alpha_{2}\multimap\alpha_{2},2)
.

Since the Quantitative Type Inference Algorithm only differs from the algorithm in
section 3
on the addition of the quantitative measure, and only infers a linear rank 2 intersection type and not a multi-type, the typing soundness (
Theorem 54
) and completeness (
Theorem 55
) are formalized in a similar way.

Theorem 54

(Typing soundness)
.

If
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=([x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\vec{\tau}_{n}],\sigma,b)
, then
[x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\dots,x_{n}:(\vec{\tau}_{n},\vec{t}_{n})]\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M:(\sigma,s)
(for some measure
b^{\prime}
and multi-types
s,\vec{t}_{1},\dots,\vec{t}_{n}
).

Proof.

The proof follows as in
Theorem 32
(only the non-t-indexed rules are necessary).
∎

Theorem 55

(Typing completeness)
.

If
\Phi\rhd[x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\dots,x_{n}:(\vec{\tau}_{n},\vec{t}_{n})]\vdash^{b}M:(\sigma,s)
, then
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=(\Gamma^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime},b^{\prime})
(for some
\mathbb{T_{L}}_{1}
-environment
\Gamma^{\prime}
, type
\sigma^{\prime}
and measure
b^{\prime}
) and there is a substitution
\mathbb{S}
such that
\mathbb{S}(\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma
and
\mathbb{S}(\Gamma^{\prime})\equiv[x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\vec{\tau}_{n}]
.

Proof.

The proof follows similarly to the proof of
Theorem 35
(note that even when t-indexed rules are used in the derivation, the resulting linear rank 2 intersection type is the same as when the correspondent non-t-indexed rules are used).
∎

As for the quantitative measure given by the algorithm, we conjecture that it corresponds to the number of evaluation steps of the typed term to normal form, when using the leftmost-outermost evaluation strategy. We strongly believe the conjecture holds, based on the attempted proofs so far and because it holds for every experimental results obtained by our implementation. We have not yet proven this property, which we formalize, in part, in the second point of the strong soundness:

Conjecture 56

(Strong soundness)
.

If
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)=([x_{1}:\vec{\tau}_{1},\dots,x_{n}:\vec{\tau}_{n}],\sigma,b)
, then:

1.

There is a derivation
\Phi\rhd[x_{1}:(\vec{\tau}_{1},\vec{t}_{1}),\dots,x_{n}:(\vec{\tau}_{n},\vec{t}_{n})]\vdash^{b^{\prime}}M:(\sigma,s)
(for some measure
b^{\prime}
and multi-types
s,\vec{t}_{1},\dots,\vec{t}_{n}
);

2.

If
\Phi
is a tight derivation, then
b=b^{\prime}
.

Note that the second point implies, by
Theorem 49
, that there exists
N
such that
M\longrightarrow^{b}N
and
\mathsf{normal}(N)
, which is what we conjecture.

We believe that proving this conjecture is not a trivial task. A first approach could be to try to use induction on the definition of
\mathsf{T_{Q}}(M)
. However, this does not work because the subderivations within a tight derivation are not necessarily tight. For that same reason, it is also not trivial to construct a tight derivation from the result given by the algorithm or from a non-tight derivation.
Thus, in order to prove this conjecture, we believe that it will be necessary to establish a stronger relation between the algorithm and tight derivations.

5
Conclusions and Future Work

When developing a non-idempotent intersection type system capable of obtaining quantitative information about a
\lambda
-term while inferring its type, we realized that the classical notion of rank was not a proper fit for non-idempotent intersection types, and that the ranks could be quantitatively more useful if the base case was changed to types that give more quantitative information in comparison to simple types, which is the case for linear types. We then came up with a new definition of rank for intersection types based on linear types, which we call
linear rank

[
23
]
. Based on the notion of linear rank, we defined a new intersection type system for the
\lambda
-calculus, restricted to linear rank 2 non-idempotent intersection types, and a new type inference algorithm which we proved to be sound and complete with respect to the type system.

We then merged that intersection type system with the system for the leftmost-outermost evaluation strategy presented in
[
1
]
in order to use the linear rank 2 non-idempotent intersection types to obtain quantitative information about the typed terms, and we proved that the resulting type system gives the correct number of evaluation steps for a kind of derivations. We also extended the type inference algorithm we had defined, in order to also give that measure, and showed that it is sound and complete with respect to the type system for the inferred types, and conjectured that the inferred measures correspond to the ones given by the type system.

In the future, we would like to:

•

prove
56
;

•

further explore the relation between our definition of linear rank and the classical definition of rank;

•

extend the type systems and the type inference algorithms for the affine terms;

•

adapt the Linear Rank 2 Quantitative Type System and the Quantitative Type Inference Algorithm for other evaluation strategies.
